   590→
   591→## Task 7: PostgreSQL Release Store (Transactional Outbox Implementation)
   592→
   593→**Files:**
   594→- Create: `internal/ingestion/pgstore.go`
   595→
   596→This is the real `ReleaseStore` implementation. It performs the transactional outbox: BEGIN → INSERT release → River InsertTx job → COMMIT. No unit test here — it's a thin infrastructure adapter best validated by integration tests. The interface mock in Task 6 covers the service logic.
   597→
   598→**Step 1: Implement PgStore**
   599→
   600→```go
   601→// internal/ingestion/pgstore.go
   602→package ingestion
   603→
   604→import (
   605→	"context"
   606→	"encoding/json"
   607→	"fmt"
   608→
   609→	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
   610→	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
   611→	"github.com/riverqueue/river"
   612→	"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/models"
   613→	"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/queue"
   614→)
   615→
   616→// PgStore implements ReleaseStore using PostgreSQL + River for the transactional outbox.
   617→type PgStore struct {
   618→	pool  *pgxpool.Pool
   619→	river *river.Client[pgx.Tx]
   620→}
   621→
   622→func NewPgStore(pool *pgxpool.Pool, riverClient *river.Client[pgx.Tx]) *PgStore {
   623→	return &PgStore{pool: pool, river: riverClient}
   624→}
   625→
   626→// IngestRelease inserts a release and enqueues a pipeline job in a single transaction.
   627→// Returns an error on unique constraint violation (caller treats as idempotent skip).
   628→func (s *PgStore) IngestRelease(ctx context.Context, event *models.ReleaseEvent) error {
   629→	tx, err := s.pool.Begin(ctx)
   630→	if err != nil {
   631→		return fmt.Errorf("begin tx: %w", err)
   632→	}
   633→	defer tx.Rollback(ctx)
   634→
   635→	payload, err := json.Marshal(event)
   636→	if err != nil {
   637→		return fmt.Errorf("marshal payload: %w", err)
   638→	}
   639→
   640→	_, err = tx.Exec(ctx,
   641→		`INSERT INTO releases (id, source, repository, version, payload) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5)`,
   642→		event.ID, event.Source, event.Repository, event.RawVersion, payload,
   643→	)
   644→	if err != nil {
   645→		return fmt.Errorf("insert release: %w", err)
   646→	}
   647→
   648→	_, err = s.river.InsertTx(ctx, tx, queue.PipelineJobArgs{ReleaseID: event.ID}, nil)
   649→	if err != nil {
   650→		return fmt.Errorf("enqueue job: %w", err)
   651→	}
   652→
   653→	if err := tx.Commit(ctx); err != nil {
   654→		return fmt.Errorf("commit: %w", err)
   655→	}
   656→	return nil
   657→}
   658→```
   659→
   660→**Step 2: Verify build**
   661→
   662→Run: `go build ./internal/ingestion/`
   663→Expected: Success
   664→
   665→**Step 3: Commit**
   666→
   667→```bash
   668→git add internal/ingestion/pgstore.go
   669→git commit -m "feat: add PostgreSQL release store with transactional outbox"
   670→```
   671→

   759→
   760→**Step 3: Implement Docker Hub source**
   761→
   762→Polls `GET /v2/repositories/{repo}/tags/?page_size=25&ordering=last_updated`. Relies on the DB unique constraint for deduplication — no cursor tracking needed.
   763→
   764→```go
   765→// internal/ingestion/dockerhub.go
   766→package ingestion
   767→
   768→import (
   769→	"context"
   770→	"encoding/json"
   771→	"fmt"
   772→	"net/http"
   773→	"time"
   774→)
   775→
   776→const defaultDockerHubURL = "https://hub.docker.com"
   777→
   778→// DockerHubSource polls Docker Hub for new image tags.
   779→type DockerHubSource struct {
   780→	client     *http.Client
   781→	repository string
   782→	baseURL    string
   783→}
   784→
   785→func NewDockerHubSource(client *http.Client, repository string) *DockerHubSource {
   786→	return &DockerHubSource{
   787→		client:     client,
   788→		repository: repository,
   789→		baseURL:    defaultDockerHubURL,
   790→	}
   791→}
   792→
   793→func (s *DockerHubSource) Name() string { return "dockerhub" }
   794→
   795→func (s *DockerHubSource) FetchNewReleases(ctx context.Context) ([]IngestionResult, error) {
   796→	url := fmt.Sprintf("%s/v2/repositories/%s/tags/?page_size=25&ordering=last_updated",
   797→		s.baseURL, s.repository)
   798→
   799→	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, url, nil)
   800→	if err != nil {
   801→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create request: %w", err)
   802→	}
   803→
   804→	resp, err := s.client.Do(req)
   805→	if err != nil {
   806→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("fetch tags: %w", err)
   807→	}
   808→	defer resp.Body.Close()
   809→
   810→	if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
   811→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unexpected status: %d", resp.StatusCode)
   812→	}
   813→
   814→	var body struct {
   815→		Results []struct {
   816→			Name        string `json:"name"`
   817→			LastUpdated string `json:"last_updated"`
   818→		} `json:"results"`
   819→	}
   820→	if err := json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(&body); err != nil {
   821→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("decode response: %w", err)
   822→	}
   823→
   824→	results := make([]IngestionResult, 0, len(body.Results))
   825→	for _, tag := range body.Results {
   826→		ts, _ := time.Parse(time.RFC3339Nano, tag.LastUpdated)
   827→		results = append(results, IngestionResult{
   828→			Repository: s.repository,
   829→			RawVersion: tag.Name,
   830→			Timestamp:  ts,
   831→		})
   832→	}
   833→	return results, nil
   834→}
   835→```
   836→

   985→**Step 3: Implement GitHub webhook handler**
   986→
   987→```go
   988→// internal/ingestion/github.go
   989→package ingestion
   990→
   991→import (
   992→	"crypto/hmac"
   993→	"crypto/sha256"
   994→	"encoding/hex"
   995→	"encoding/json"
   996→	"io"
   997→	"net/http"
   998→	"strings"
   999→	"time"
  1000→)
  1001→
  1002→// GitHubWebhookHandler handles incoming GitHub release webhook events.
  1003→// It validates the HMAC signature, parses the release payload, and
  1004→// forwards results via the onResult callback.
  1005→type GitHubWebhookHandler struct {
  1006→	secret   string
  1007→	onResult func([]IngestionResult)
  1008→}
  1009→
  1010→func NewGitHubWebhookHandler(secret string, onResult func([]IngestionResult)) *GitHubWebhookHandler {
  1011→	return &GitHubWebhookHandler{secret: secret, onResult: onResult}
  1012→}
  1013→
  1014→func (h *GitHubWebhookHandler) ServeHTTP(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
  1015→	body, err := io.ReadAll(r.Body)
  1016→	if err != nil {
  1017→		http.Error(w, "read body", http.StatusBadRequest)
  1018→		return
  1019→	}
  1020→
  1021→	if !h.verifySignature(body, r.Header.Get("X-Hub-Signature-256")) {
  1022→		http.Error(w, "invalid signature", http.StatusForbidden)
  1023→		return
  1024→	}
  1025→
  1026→	if r.Header.Get("X-GitHub-Event") != "release" {
  1027→		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
  1028→		return
  1029→	}
  1030→
  1031→	var payload struct {
  1032→		Action  string `json:"action"`
  1033→		Release struct {
  1034→			TagName     string `json:"tag_name"`
  1035→			Body        string `json:"body"`
  1036→			PreRelease  bool   `json:"prerelease"`
  1037→			PublishedAt string `json:"published_at"`
  1038→		} `json:"release"`
  1039→		Repository struct {
  1040→			FullName string `json:"full_name"`
  1041→		} `json:"repository"`
  1042→	}
  1043→	if err := json.Unmarshal(body, &payload); err != nil {
  1044→		http.Error(w, "invalid json", http.StatusBadRequest)
  1045→		return
  1046→	}
  1047→
  1048→	if payload.Action != "published" {
  1049→		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
  1050→		return
  1051→	}
  1052→
  1053→	ts, _ := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, payload.Release.PublishedAt)
  1054→	result := IngestionResult{
  1055→		Repository: payload.Repository.FullName,
  1056→		RawVersion: payload.Release.TagName,
  1057→		Changelog:  payload.Release.Body,
  1058→		Timestamp:  ts,
  1059→	}
  1060→
  1061→	h.onResult([]IngestionResult{result})
  1062→	w.WriteHeader(http.StatusOK)
  1063→}
  1064→
  1065→func (h *GitHubWebhookHandler) verifySignature(body []byte, header string) bool {
  1066→	sig := strings.TrimPrefix(header, "sha256=")
  1067→	mac := hmac.New(sha256.New, []byte(h.secret))
  1068→	mac.Write(body)
  1069→	expected := hex.EncodeToString(mac.Sum(nil))
  1070→	return hmac.Equal([]byte(sig), []byte(expected))
  1071→}
  1072→```
  1073→
  1074→**Step 4: Run test to verify it passes**
  1075→
  1076→Run: `go test ./internal/ingestion/ -v -run TestGitHub`
  1077→Expected: PASS

  1172→**Step 3: Implement orchestrator**
  1173→
  1174→```go
  1175→// internal/ingestion/orchestrator.go
  1176→package ingestion
  1177→
  1178→import (
  1179→	"context"
  1180→	"log/slog"
  1181→	"time"
  1182→)
  1183→
  1184→// Orchestrator runs polling ingestion sources on a fixed interval.
  1185→type Orchestrator struct {
  1186→	service  *Service
  1187→	sources  []IIngestionSource
  1188→	interval time.Duration
  1189→}
  1190→
  1191→func NewOrchestrator(service *Service, sources []IIngestionSource, interval time.Duration) *Orchestrator {
  1192→	return &Orchestrator{service: service, sources: sources, interval: interval}
  1193→}
  1194→
  1195→// Run polls all sources immediately, then on every tick. Blocks until ctx is cancelled.
  1196→func (o *Orchestrator) Run(ctx context.Context) {
  1197→	ticker := time.NewTicker(o.interval)
  1198→	defer ticker.Stop()
  1199→
  1200→	o.pollAll(ctx)
  1201→
  1202→	for {
  1203→		select {
  1204→		case <-ctx.Done():
  1205→			return
  1206→		case <-ticker.C:
  1207→			o.pollAll(ctx)
  1208→		}
  1209→	}
  1210→}
  1211→
  1212→func (o *Orchestrator) pollAll(ctx context.Context) {
  1213→	for _, src := range o.sources {
  1214→		results, err := src.FetchNewReleases(ctx)
  1215→		if err != nil {
  1216→			slog.Error("poll failed", "source", src.Name(), "err", err)
  1217→			continue
  1218→		}
  1219→		if len(results) == 0 {
  1220→			continue
  1221→		}
  1222→		if err := o.service.ProcessResults(ctx, src.Name(), results); err != nil {
  1223→			slog.Error("process failed", "source", src.Name(), "err", err)
  1224→		}
  1225→	}
  1226→}
  1227→```
  1228→

  1230→
  1231→Run: `go test ./internal/ingestion/ -v -run TestOrchestrator`
  1232→Expected: PASS

  1243→## Task 11: Wire Up main.go
  1244→
  1245→**Files:**
  1246→- Modify: `cmd/server/main.go`
  1247→
  1248→**Step 1: Connect all components**
  1249→
  1250→```go
  1251→// cmd/server/main.go
  1252→package main
  1253→
  1254→import (
  1255→	"context"
  1256→	"log/slog"
  1257→	"net/http"
  1258→	"os"
  1259→	"os/signal"
  1260→	"time"
  1261→
  1262→	"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/db"
  1263→	"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/ingestion"
  1264→	"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/queue"
  1265→)
  1266→
  1267→func main() {
  1268→	ctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), os.Interrupt)
  1269→	defer stop()
  1270→
  1271→	dbURL := envOr("DATABASE_URL", "postgres://localhost:5432/releaseguard?sslmode=disable")
  1272→	ghSecret := envOr("GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET", "")
  1273→
  1274→	// Database
  1275→	pool, err := db.NewPool(ctx, dbURL)
  1276→	if err != nil {
  1277→		slog.Error("database connection failed", "err", err)
  1278→		os.Exit(1)
  1279→	}
  1280→	defer pool.Close()
  1281→
  1282→	if err := db.RunMigrations(ctx, pool); err != nil {
  1283→		slog.Error("migrations failed", "err", err)
  1284→		os.Exit(1)
  1285→	}
  1286→
  1287→	// River queue (insert-only — no workers registered yet)
  1288→	riverClient, err := queue.NewRiverClient(pool, nil)
  1289→	if err != nil {
  1290→		slog.Error("river client failed", "err", err)
  1291→		os.Exit(1)
  1292→	}
  1293→
  1294→	// Ingestion layer
  1295→	store := ingestion.NewPgStore(pool, riverClient)
  1296→	svc := ingestion.NewService(store)
  1297→
  1298→	sources := []ingestion.IIngestionSource{
  1299→		ingestion.NewDockerHubSource(http.DefaultClient, "library/golang"),
  1300→	}
  1301→
  1302→	orch := ingestion.NewOrchestrator(svc, sources, 5*time.Minute)
  1303→
  1304→	// GitHub webhook handler
  1305→	webhookHandler := ingestion.NewGitHubWebhookHandler(ghSecret, func(results []ingestion.IngestionResult) {
  1306→		if err := svc.ProcessResults(ctx, "github", results); err != nil {
  1307→			slog.Error("github webhook processing failed", "err", err)
  1308→		}
  1309→	})
  1310→
  1311→	mux := http.NewServeMux()
  1312→	mux.Handle("POST /webhook/github", webhookHandler)
  1313→
  1314→	srv := &http.Server{Addr: ":8080", Handler: mux}
  1315→
  1316→	// Start polling in background
  1317→	go orch.Run(ctx)
  1318→
  1319→	// Start HTTP server
  1320→	go func() {
  1321→		slog.Info("server starting", "addr", srv.Addr)
  1322→		if err := srv.ListenAndServe(); err != http.ErrServerClosed {
  1323→			slog.Error("server error", "err", err)
  1324→		}
  1325→	}()
  1326→
  1327→	<-ctx.Done()
  1328→	slog.Info("shutting down")
  1329→	srv.Shutdown(context.Background())
  1330→}
  1331→
  1332→func envOr(key, fallback string) string {
  1333→	if v := os.Getenv(key); v != "" {
  1334→		return v
  1335→	}
  1336→	return fallback
  1337→}
  1338→```
  1339→

A `Runner` orchestrates execution: always-on nodes (Regex Normalizer, Subscription Router) run for every release; configurable nodes (Urgency Scorer, etc.) run only when their key is present in `pipeline_config`. Each node implements the `PipelineNode` interface, receives prior results and config as raw JSON. A `pipeline_jobs` table tracks `current_node`, `node_results`, and `state` for dashboard observability. Without the `projects` table (not yet built), configurable nodes are dormant — only always-on nodes execute.\n\n**Tech Stack:** Go 1.25, PostgreSQL (pgx/v5), River v0.31.0, regexp, (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\n\t\"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/models\"\n)\n\n// Store provides data access for the pipeline runner.\ntype Store interface {\n\tGetReleasePayload(ctx context.Context, releaseID string) (*models.ReleaseEvent, error)\n\tUpsertPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, releaseID string) (int64, error)\n\tUpdateNodeProgress(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, currentNode string, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error\n\tCompletePipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error\n\tSkipPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, reason string) error\n\tFailPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, errMsg string) error\n}\n```\n\n**Step 2: Implement PgStore**\n\n```go\n// internal/pipeline/pgstore.go\npackage pipeline\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\n\t\"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool\"\n\t\"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/models\"\n)\n\n// PgStore implements Store and SubscriptionChecker using PostgreSQL.\ntype PgStore struct {\n\tpool *pgxpool.Pool\n}\n\nfunc NewPgStore(pool *pgxpool.Pool) *PgStore {\n\treturn &PgStore{pool: pool}\n}\n\nfunc (s *PgStore) GetReleasePayload(ctx context.Context, releaseID string) (*models.ReleaseEvent, error) {\n\tvar payload []byte\n\terr := s.pool.QueryRow(ctx, `SELECT payload FROM releases WHERE id = $1`, releaseID).Scan(&payload)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"get release: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tvar event models.ReleaseEvent\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(payload, &event); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"unmarshal payload: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn &event, nil\n}\n\nfunc (s *PgStore) UpsertPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, releaseID string) (int64, error) {\n\tvar id int64\n\terr := s.pool.QueryRow(ctx, `\n\t\tINSERT INTO pipeline_jobs (release_id, state)\n\t\tVALUES ($1, 'running')\n\t\tON CONFLICT (release_id) DO UPDATE SET\n\t\t\tstate = 'running',\n\t\t\tcurrent_node = NULL,\n\t\t\tnode_results = '{}',\n\t\t\terror_message = NULL,\n\t\t\tcompleted_at = NULL\n\t\tRETURNING id\n\t`, releaseID).Scan(&id)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn 0, fmt.Errorf(\"upsert pipeline job: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn id, nil\n}\n\nfunc (s *PgStore) UpdateNodeProgress(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, currentNode string, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error {\n\tresultsJSON, err := json.Marshal(nodeResults)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"marshal results: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\t_, err = s.pool.Exec(ctx,\n\t\t`UPDATE pipeline_jobs SET current_node = $2, node_results = $3 WHERE id = $1`,\n\t\tjobID, currentNode, resultsJSON,\n\t)\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc (s *PgStore) CompletePipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error {\n\tresultsJSON, err := json.Marshal(nodeResults)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"marshal results: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\t_, err = s.pool.Exec(ctx,\n\t\t`UPDATE pipeline_jobs SET state = 'completed', current_node = NULL, node_results = $2, completed_at = NOW() WHERE id = $1`,\n\t\tjobID, resultsJSON,\n\t)\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc (s *PgStore) SkipPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, reason string) error {\n\t_, err := s.pool.Exec(ctx,\n\t\t`UPDATE pipeline_jobs SET state = 'skipped', error_message = $2, completed_at = NOW() WHERE id = $1`,\n\t\tjobID, reason,\n\t)\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc (s *PgStore) FailPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, errMsg string) error {\n\t_, err := s.pool.Exec(ctx,\n\t\t`UPDATE pipeline_jobs SET state = 'failed', error_message = $2, completed_at = NOW() WHERE id = $1`,\n\t\tjobID, errMsg,\n\t)\n\treturn err\n}\n\n// HasSubscribers returns true if the repository has active subscriptions,\n// or if no subscriptions exist at all (default open — system unconfigured).\nfunc (s *PgStore) HasSubscribers(ctx context.Context, repository string) (bool, error) {\n\tvar result bool\n\terr := s.pool.QueryRow(ctx, `\n\t\tSELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM subscriptions WHERE repository = $1)\n\t\t\tOR NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM subscriptions)\n\t`, repository).Scan(&result)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, fmt.Errorf(\"check subscribers: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn result, nil\n}\n```\n\n**Step 3: Run test to verify it fails**\n\nRun: `go test ./internal/pipeline/ -v -run TestUrgencyScorer`\nExpected: FAIL — `NewUrgencyScorer` not defined\n\n**Step 3: Implement the Urgency Scorer**\n\n```go\n// internal/pipeline/urgency_scorer.go\npackage pipeline\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\n\t\"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/models\"\n)\n\nvar securityKeywords = []string{\"security\", \"cve\", \"vulnerability\", \"critical\", \"exploit\", \"rce\", \"injection\"}\n\n// UrgencyScorerResult is the output of the Urgency Scorer node.\ntype UrgencyScorerResult struct {\n\tScore   string   `json:\"score\"`\n\tFactors []string `json:\"factors\"`\n}\n\n// UrgencyScorer computes a composite urgency level from prior node results and\n// the release changelog. Configurable node — only runs when enabled in pipeline_config.\ntype UrgencyScorer struct{}\n\nfunc NewUrgencyScorer() *UrgencyScorer { return &UrgencyScorer{} }\n\nfunc (n *UrgencyScorer) Name() string { return \"urgency_scorer\" }\n\nfunc (n *UrgencyScorer) Execute(_ context.Context, event *models.ReleaseEvent, _ json.RawMessage, prior map[string]json.RawMessage) (json.RawMessage, error) {\n\tvar normResult RegexNormalizerResult\n\tif raw, ok := prior[\"regex_normalizer\"]; ok {\n\t\tjson.Unmarshal(raw, &normResult)\n\t}\n\n\tscore := \"MEDIUM\"\n\tvar factors []string\n\n\t// Security keywords override everything\n\tchangelogLower := strings.ToLower(event.Changelog)\n\tfor _, kw := range securityKeywords {\n\t\tif strings.Contains(changelogLower, kw) {\n\t\t\tscore = \"CRITICAL\"\n\t\t\tfactors = append(factors, \"security_keyword_\"+kw)\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif score != \"CRITICAL\" {\n\t\tswitch {\n\t\tcase normResult.IsPreRelease:\n\t\t\tscore = \"LOW\"\n\t\t\tfactors = append(factors, \"pre_release\")\n\t\tcase normResult.Parsed && normResult.SemanticVersion.Minor == 0 && normResult.SemanticVersion.Patch == 0:\n\t\t\tscore = \"HIGH\"\n\t\t\tfactors = append(factors, \"major_version\")\n\t\tcase normResult.Parsed && normResult.SemanticVersion.Patch > 0:\n\t\t\tscore = \"LOW\"\n\t\t\tfactors = append(factors, \"patch_version\")\n\t\tdefault:\n\t\t\tfactors = append(factors, \"minor_version\")\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn json.Marshal(UrgencyScorerResult{Score: score, Factors: factors})\n}\n\nvar _ PipelineNode = (*UrgencyScorer)(nil)\n```\n\n**Step 4: Run test to verify it passes**\n\nRun: `go test ./internal/pipeline/ -v -run TestUrgencyScorer`\nExpected: PASS\n\n**Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add internal/pipeline/urgency_scorer.go internal/pipeline/urgency_scorer_test.go\ngit commit -m \"feat: add Urgency Scorer pipeline node\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Task 7: Pipeline Runner\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `internal/pipeline/runner.go`\n- Test: `internal/pipeline/runner_test.go`\n\nThe Runner is the core engine that orchestrates node execution. 3: Implement the Runner**\n\n```go\n// internal/pipeline/runner.go\npackage pipeline\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n)\n\n// Runner orchestrates DAG pipeline execution for a single release event.\ntype Runner struct {\n\tstore        Store\n\talwaysOn     []PipelineNode\n\tconfigurable []PipelineNode\n}\n\nfunc NewRunner(store Store, alwaysOn []PipelineNode, configurable []PipelineNode) *Runner {\n\treturn &Runner{store: store, alwaysOn: alwaysOn, configurable: configurable}\n}\n\n// Process runs the full pipeline for a release. pipelineConfig controls which\n// configurable nodes are enabled (nil = only always-on nodes run).\nfunc (r *Runner) Process(ctx context.Context, releaseID string, pipelineConfig map[string]json.RawMessage) error {\n\tevent, err := r.store.GetReleasePayload(ctx, releaseID)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"get release: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tjobID, err := r.store.UpsertPipelineJob(ctx, releaseID)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"create pipeline job: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tnodeResults := make(map[string]json.RawMessage)\n\n\t// Always-on nodes\n\tfor _, node := range r.alwaysOn {\n\t\tif err := r.runNode(ctx, jobID, node, event, nil, nodeResults); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Configurable nodes — only enabled ones\n\tfor _, node := range r.configurable {\n\t\tconfig, enabled := pipelineConfig[node.Name()]\n\t\tif !enabled {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := r.runNode(ctx, jobID, node, event, config, nodeResults); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn r.store.CompletePipelineJob(ctx, jobID, nodeResults)\n}\n\nfunc (r *Runner) runNode(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, node PipelineNode, event *models.ReleaseEvent, config json.RawMessage, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error {\n\tr.store.UpdateNodeProgress(ctx, jobID, node.Name(), nodeResults)\n\n\tresult, err := node.Execute(ctx, event, config, nodeResults)\n\tif errors.Is(err, ErrEventDropped) {\n\t\treturn r.store.SkipPipelineJob(ctx, jobID, fmt.Sprintf(\"dropped by %s\", node.Name()))\n\t}\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tr.store.FailPipelineJob(ctx, jobID, err.Error())\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %s: %w\", node.Name(), err)\n\t}\n\n\tnodeResults[node.Name()] = result\n\treturn nil\n}\n```\n\nNote: `runNode` needs access to `models` for the event type — add the import:\n\n```go\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\n\t\"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/models\"\n)\n```\n\n**Step 4: Run test to verify it passes**\n\nRun: `go test ./internal/pipeline/ -v -run TestRunner`\nExpected: PASS\n\n**Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add internal/pipeline/runner.go internal/pipeline/runner_test.go\ngit commit -m \"feat: add pipeline runner engine\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Task 8: River Worker\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `internal/pipeline/worker.go`\n- Test: `internal/pipeline/worker_test.go`\n\nThin wrapper that connects River's job Worker struct {\n\triver.WorkerDefaults[queue.PipelineJobArgs]\n\trunner *Runner\n}\n\nfunc NewWorker(runner *Runner) *Worker {\n\treturn &Worker{runner: runner}\n}\n\nfunc (w *Worker) Work(ctx commit -m \"feat: add River worker for pipeline job processing\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Task 9: Wire Pipeline into main.go\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `cmd/server/main.go`\n\n**Step 1: Update main.go to create pipeline components and start River with workers**\n\nReplace the current `main()` function with:\n\n```go\npackage main\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"log/slog\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"os\"\n\t\"os/signal\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/riverqueue/river\"\n\t\"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/db\"\n\t\"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/ingestion\"\n\t\"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/pipeline\"\n\t\"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/queue\"\n)\n\nfunc main() {\n\tctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), os.Interrupt)\n\tdefer stop()\n\n\tdbURL := envOr(\"DATABASE_URL\", \"postgres://localhost:5432/releaseguard?sslmode=disable\")\n\tghSecret := envOr(\"GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET\", \"\")\n\taddr := envOr(\"LISTEN_ADDR\", \":8080\")\n\n\t// Database\n\tpool, err := db.NewPool(ctx, dbURL)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tslog.Error(\"database connection failed\", \"err\", err)\n\t\tos.Exit(1)\n\t}\n\tdefer pool.Close()\n\n\tif err := db.RunMigrations(ctx, pool); err != nil {\n\t\tslog.Error(\"migrations failed\", \"err\", err)\n\t\tos.Exit(1)\n\t}\n\n\t// Pipeline\n\tpipelineStore := pipeline.NewPgStore(pool)\n\tpipelineRunner := pipeline.NewRunner(\n\t\tpipelineStore,\n\t\t[]pipeline.PipelineNode{\n\t\t\tpipeline.NewRegexNormalizer(),\n\t\t\tpipeline.NewSubscriptionRouter(pipelineStore),\n\t\t},\n\t\t[]pipeline.PipelineNode{\n\t\t\tpipeline.NewUrgencyScorer(),\n\t\t},\n\t)\n\n\t// River queue with pipeline worker\n\tworkers := river.NewWorkers()\n\triver.AddWorker(workers, pipeline.NewWorker(pipelineRunner))\n\n\triverClient, err := queue.NewRiverClient(pool, workers)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tslog.Error(\"river client failed\", \"err\", err)\n\t\tos.Exit(1)\n\t}\n\n\tif err := riverClient.Start(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\tslog.Error(\"river start failed\", \"err\", err)\n\t\tos.Exit(1)\n\t}\n\n\t// Ingestion layer\n\tingestStore := ingestion.NewPgStore(pool, riverClient)\n\tsvc := ingestion.NewService(ingestStore)\n\n\tsources := []ingestion.IIngestionSource{\n\t\tingestion.NewDockerHubSource(http.DefaultClient, \"library/golang\"),\n\t}\n\n\torch := ingestion.NewOrchestrator(svc, sources, 5*time.Minute)\n\n\t// GitHub webhook handler\n\twebhookHandler := ingestion.NewGitHubWebhookHandler(ghSecret, func(results []ingestion.IngestionResult) {\n\t\tif err := svc.ProcessResults(ctx, \"github\", results); err != nil {\n\t\t\tslog.Error(\"github webhook processing failed\", \"err\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t})\n\n\tmux := http.NewServeMux()\n\tmux.Handle(\"POST /webhook/github\", webhookHandler)\n\n\tsrv := &http.Server{Addr: addr, Handler: mux}\n\n\t// Start polling in background\n\tgo orch.Run(ctx)\n\n\t// Start HTTP server\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tslog.Info(\"server starting\", \"addr\", srv.Addr)\n\t\tif err := srv.ListenAndServe(); err != http.ErrServerClosed {\n\t\t\tslog.Error(\"server error\", \"err\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\n\t<-ctx.Done()\n\tslog.Info(\"shutting down\")\n\n\tshutdownCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)\n\tdefer cancel()\n\triverClient.Stop(shutdownCtx)\n\tsrv.Shutdown(shutdownCtx)\n}\n\nfunc envOr(key, fallback string) string {\n\tif v := os.Getenv(key); v != \"\" {\n\t\treturn v\n\t}\n\treturn fallback\n}\n```\n\nKey changes from current `main.go`:\n- Added `pipeline` and `river` imports\n- Created pipeline store, runner, and nodes\n- > AGENT

Plan complete and saved to [docs/plans/2026-02-24-dag-pipeline.md](docs/plans/2026-02-24-dag-pipeline.md).

**Summary — 10 tasks, TDD throughout:**

| Task | Component | Type |
|------|-----------|------|
| 1 | `pipeline_jobs` table schema | Migration |
| 2 | `PipelineNode` interface + `ErrEventDropped` | Interface |
| 3 | `Store` interface + `PgStore` (release lookup, job tracking, subscription check) | Infrastructure |
| 4 | **Regex Normalizer** — parses semver, sets `IsPreRelease`, mutates event | Always-on node |
| 5 | **Subscription Router** — drops events with no subscribers | Always-on node |
| 6 | **Urgency Scorer** — heuristic urgency from version + changelog keywords | Configurable node |
| 7 | **Runner** — orchestrates always-on → configurable nodes, tracks progress | Core engine |
| 8 | **River Worker** — thin bridge from River jobs to Runner | Worker |
| 9 | Wire into `main.go` — start River with workers | Integration |
| 10 | Full verification — all tests, vet, build | Verification |

**Key design decisions:**
- Without `projects` table (not yet built), configurable nodes are dormant — only always-on nodes execute
- `pipeline_jobs` table tracks `current_node` and `node_results` for dashboard observability
- `SubscriptionChecker.HasSubscribers` defaults to "pass" when no subscriptions exist (system unconfigured)
- Nodes receive `prior` results as `map[string]json.RawMessage` for loose coupling
- Regex Normalizer mutates the event so downstream nodes can read `event.SemanticVersion` directly

**Two execution options:**

1. **Subagent-Driven (this session)** — I dispatch a fresh subagent per task, review between tasks, fast iteration

2. **Parallel Session (separate)** — Open new session with `executing-plans`, batch execution with checkpoints

Which approach?

> AGENT

Good question. Let me check what the architecture specifies about node execution order.

The design is explicit about this — from [ARCH.md:129-147](ARCH.md#L129-L147):

> The IR payload is passed through these nodes **sequentially**.

And from [DESIGN.md:39](DESIGN.md#L39):

> The IR payload is passed through these nodes **sequentially**. This compiler-like approach...

**The DAG is sequential by design — no parallel node execution.** Here's why that's the right call:

**Data dependencies make parallelism impractical for most nodes:**
- Subscription Router needs Regex Normalizer's output (to know if it's a pre-release)
- Urgency Scorer reads from *all* preceding node results
- Validation Trigger depends on the urgency score
- Each node can mutate the `ReleaseEvent` (normalizer sets `SemanticVersion`, `IsPreRelease`)

**No race conditions in the current plan** because:
1. One River worker processes one release's pipeline at a time (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`)
2. Nodes execute sequentially within that single goroutine
3. The shared `nodeResults` map is written after each node returns, never concurrently
4. `pipeline_jobs` row is updated serially (`current_node`, `node_results`)

The `UNIQUE(release_id)` constraint on `pipeline_jobs` also prevents two workers from processing the same release simultaneously.

**Could some nodes theoretically run in parallel?** Yes — Availability Checker and Risk Assessor are independent of each other. But the added complexity of fan-out/fan-in synchronization isn't worth it for nodes that complete in milliseconds (or a few seconds for LLM calls). The bottleneck is external I/O (Docker Hub API, LLM), not CPU.

No changes needed to the plan — sequential execution is what the architecture specifies and the plan implements.

> DEVELOPER

Then what's the meaning of DAG?

> AGENT

You're right to call that out. Honestly — what the architecture describes and what the plan implements is a **linear pipeline with optional nodes**, not a real DAG.

A true DAG would have **branching and fan-in**. Looking at the actual data dependencies between nodes, there IS a natural DAG shape:

```
Regex Normalizer
       ↓
Subscription Router
       ↓
  ┌────┼────────────┬──────────────┐
  ↓    ↓            ↓              ↓
Avail. Risk      Adoption    Changelog
Check  Assessor  Tracker     Summarizer
  └────┼────────────┴──────────────┘
       ↓
 Urgency Scorer  (reads ALL prior results)
       ↓
Validation Trigger
```

The middle four nodes are **independent** — they don't read each other's output. They could run concurrently and fan back in at the Urgency Scorer.

So there are two options:

1. **Be honest about the linear design** — rename it "configurable pipeline" in the docs, keep sequential execution, the plan stays as-is
2. **Make it a real DAG** — nodes declare their dependencies, the runner resolves execution order, independent nodes fan out via goroutines, fan in with a sync point before dependent nodes

Option 2 is more complex (goroutine fan-out, error propagation, partial failure handling) but earns the "DAG" name and gives real parallelism for I/O-bound nodes like LLM calls and API checks.

Which direction do you want to take? This would change the Runner design in the plan.

> DEVELOPER

Let's go with option1 first and we can optimize in the future

> AGENT

Updated the plan to rename "DAG" to "configurable pipeline" throughout.

```
CLAUDE.md:7:**ReleaseBeacon** (internal name: ReleaseGuard) is an event-driven release management system that polls upstream registries (Docker Hub, GitHub) for new releases, processes them through a DAG pipeline, and routes notifications to downstream systems (Slack, PagerDuty, Ops Opsack).
CLAUDE.md:13:- **Backend:** Go 1.25 — polling engine, DAG pipeline, API server

CLAUDE.md:67:- `PipelineNode` — DAG processing stages
CLAUDE.md:76:    → River worker picks up job → DAG pipeline processes → notifications sent
CLAUDE.md:88:- `ARCH.md` — Full architecture: system diagram, data flow lifecycle, DAG pipeline design
DESIGN.md:37:### 2.2 The DAG Processing Pipeline
DESIGN.md:39:Instead of a rigid, procedural filtering function, the event processing is designed as a **Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG)**. The IR payload is passed through these nodes sequentially. This compiler-like approach makes it trivial to add new evaluation steps or branch the logic for multiple outputs without breaking existing flows.
DESIGN.md:210:-- River-compatible job queue for the DAG pipeline
DESIGN.md:274:* **Dead-Letter Queue (DLQ):** If a DAG pipeline job fails `max_attempts` (e.g., the LLM API is down), the `pipeline_jobs` state is updated to `discarded` and `error_message` captures the failure reason. A separate Postgres trigger alerts the system admin that an event requires manual intervention.
DESIGN.md:275:* **Pipeline Observability:** Each pipeline job tracks its `current_node` and accumulates `node_results` as it progresses through the DAG. If a job fails mid-pipeline, the exact failure point and partial results are preserved for debugging.
ARCH.md:39:        PG --> |SKIP LOCKED\nRiver| DAG[DAG Processing Pipeline]
ARCH.md:40:        DAG --> |UPDATE node_results\ncurrent_node| PG
ARCH.md:44:        DAG -.-> |Trigger Tool| Agent[SRE Agent]
ARCH.md:109:* **Reliable Queues:** Go background workers poll the `pipeline_jobs` table using `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, ensuring only one worker processes a specific release's DAG pipeline at a time.
ARCH.md:129:### 2.4 The DAG Processing Pipeline
ARCH.md:131:The core filtering and scoring logic is structured as a **configurable Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG)**. Instead of monolithic conditional blocks, the system compiles a pipeline of independent execution nodes. **Which nodes run and how they behave is controlled per-project** via `pipeline_config` — an opaque JSONB map where each key is a node name and its value is that node's configuration.
ARCH.md:164:3. **Processing (Queue Pull):** A Go worker pulls the job using `SKIP LOCKED` and routes the event through the DAG pipeline. Each node updates `current_node` and accumulates results in `node_results`. The subscription router checks the parent project's subscriptions — unsubscribed events are marked as `skipped`.
```

     1→# Architecture: ReleaseGuard
     2→
     3→## Overview
     4→
     5→ReleaseGuard is an event-driven, hybrid-architecture system designed to centralize release discovery, automate validation, manage configurations, and distribute targeted notifications. It combines the high-concurrency performance of a Go-based polling engine with the reasoning capabilities of LLM-based SRE agents. By leveraging PostgreSQL for both persistent storage and message brokering, the system maintains high reliability and transactional consistency within a streamlined, single-binary deployment.
     6→
     7→## 1. Tech Stack & Infrastructure
     8→
     9→* **Backend / Engine:** Go (Golang). Chosen for its lightweight concurrency model (Goroutines) to handle simultaneous polling across multiple registries.
    10→* **Frontend / Dashboard:** Next.js (React) with Tailwind CSS. Provides a fast, modern control center for viewing release streams and managing configurations.
    11→* **Database, Queue, & Event Bus:** PostgreSQL.
    12→* *Persistent Storage:* Standard relational tables for release metadata and system configurations.
    13→* *Task Queue:* Utilizes `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (via the **River** Go library) for robust, concurrent background job processing without race conditions.
    14→* *Pub/Sub:* Utilizes native `LISTEN` and `NOTIFY` for real-time broadcasting to connected clients.
    15→
    16→
    17→* **Intelligence Layer:** LLMs (Gemini / GPT-4o-mini) orchestrated via agent frameworks (e.g., LangGraph or Claude Agent SDK) for semantic changelog analysis and autonomous validation.
    18→* **Packaging:** Single binary deployment utilizing Go's `//go:embed` to serve the Next.js static export directly from the Go server.
    19→
    20→## 2. System Design & Abstractions
    21→
    22→The system is decoupled into four primary layers communicating entirely through PostgreSQL using the Transactional Outbox pattern. A **Project** is the central domain entity — it groups multiple ingestion sources and notification subscriptions under a single tracked piece of software.
    23→
    24→```mermaid
    25→graph LR
    26→    %% Data Flow
    27→    subgraph Dashboard [API & Dashboard]
    28→        API[REST API] --> |CRUD| PG
    29→        API --> |SSE| Browser[Next.js Dashboard]
    30→    end
    31→
    32→    subgraph Ingestion [Ingestion Layer]
    33→        I_Hub(Docker Hub Poller) --> |IIngestionSource| Engine
    34→        I_Git(GitHub Webhooks) --> |HTTP Handler| Engine
    35→    end
    36→
    37→    subgraph Core [Event Bus & Queue - PostgreSQL]
    38→        Engine((Go Engine)) --> |INSERT release + job\nTransactional Outbox| PG[(PostgreSQL)]
    39→        PG --> |SKIP LOCKED\nRiver| DAG[DAG Processing Pipeline]
    40→        DAG --> |UPDATE node_results\ncurrent_node| PG
    41→    end
    42→
    43→    subgraph Intelligence [Agentic Validation]
    44→        DAG -.-> |Trigger Tool| Agent[SRE Agent]
    45→        Agent -.-> |Verify| Sandbox[(Sandbox / Base A Box)]
    46→    end
    47→
    48→    subgraph Output [Routing & Notification]
    49→        PG --> |LISTEN/NOTIFY| Router[Notification Matrix]
    50→        Router --> |INotificationChannel| O_Slack(Slack/Teams)
    51→        Router --> |INotificationChannel| O_PD(PagerDuty)
    52→        Router --> |INotificationChannel| O_Hook(Custom Webhooks)
    53→    end
    54→
    55→```
    56→
    57→### Entity Relationship Model
    58→
    59→```mermaid
    60→erDiagram
    61→    projects ||--o{ sources : "has many"
    62→    projects ||--o{ subscriptions : "has many"
    63→    sources ||--o{ releases : "produces"
    64→    releases ||--o{ pipeline_jobs : "triggers"
    65→    notification_channels ||--o{ subscriptions : "receives via"
    66→
    67→    projects {
    68→        int id PK
    69→        string name UK
    70→        string description
    71→        string url
    72→        jsonb pipeline_config
    73→    }
    74→    sources {
    75→        int id PK
    76→        int project_id FK
    77→        string source_type
    78→        string repository
    79→        int poll_interval_seconds
    80→        boolean enabled
    81→    }
    82→    releases {
    83→        uuid id PK
    84→        int source_id FK
    85→        string version
    86→        jsonb payload
    87→    }
    88→    subscriptions {
    89→        int id PK
    90→        int project_id FK
    91→        int channel_id FK
    92→        string channel_type
    93→        string frequency
    94→    }
    95→    notification_channels {
    96→        int id PK
    97→        string type
    98→        string name
    99→        jsonb config
   100→    }
   101→```
   102→
   103→### 2.1 The Event-Driven Backbone (PostgreSQL Only)
   104→
   105→Components do not call each other synchronously. Instead, they rely on PostgreSQL to guarantee delivery:
   106→
   107→* **The Transactional Outbox:** When a new release is detected, the ingestion worker writes the metadata to the `releases` table (linked to its `source_id`) and simultaneously inserts a processing job into the `pipeline_jobs` table *within the exact same SQL transaction*. This guarantees no events are ever lost.
   108→* **Real-time Pub/Sub:** Database triggers use `pg_notify` to broadcast lightweight events (e.g., telling the Next.js UI via SSE to refresh) over standard Postgres connections using `LISTEN`.
   109→* **Reliable Queues:** Go background workers poll the `pipeline_jobs` table using `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, ensuring only one worker processes a specific release's DAG pipeline at a time.
   110→* **Project-Centric Model:** All data flows through the `projects` → `sources` → `releases` hierarchy. Subscriptions attach to projects, so notification rules automatically cover releases from any of a project's sources.
   111→
   112→### 2.2 Provider Interfaces (I/O)
   113→
   114→All external integrations are abstracted behind strict Go interfaces.
   115→
   116→* `IIngestionSource`: Standardizes how polling workers fetch data. Adding a new registry (like npm or NuGet) only requires implementing this interface. Each implementation maps to a `source_type` in the database.
   117→* `INotificationChannel`: Standardizes output routing. Each implementation maps to a `type` in the `notification_channels` table (Slack, PagerDuty, custom webhooks).
   118→
   119→### 2.3 REST API & Dashboard
   120→
   121→The Go server exposes a RESTful API (`/api/v1`) serving the Next.js dashboard and external consumers:
   122→
   123→* **Resource CRUD:** Projects, sources, subscriptions, notification channels — all manageable through the API.
   124→* **Read-Only Releases:** Releases and pipeline status are queryable but not writable via the API — they're created exclusively through the ingestion layer.
   125→* **SSE Real-Time Events:** `GET /api/v1/events` streams server-sent events backed by PostgreSQL `LISTEN/NOTIFY`, pushing release and pipeline updates to connected dashboard clients.
   126→* **API Key Auth:** Bearer token authentication with hashed key storage. Webhooks use their own HMAC-based auth.
   127→* **Rate Limiting:** Per-key token bucket with standard `X-Ratelimit-*` response headers.
   128→
   129→### 2.4 The DAG Processing Pipeline
   130→
   131→The core filtering and scoring logic is structured as a **configurable Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG)**. Instead of monolithic conditional blocks, the system compiles a pipeline of independent execution nodes. **Which nodes run and how they behave is controlled per-project** via `pipeline_config` — an opaque JSONB map where each key is a node name and its value is that node's configuration.
   132→
   133→**Always-on nodes** (structural — cannot be disabled):
   134→
   135→1. **Regex Normalizer** — Parses version, applies source-level exclusion filters.
   136→2. **Subscription Router** — Checks project subscriptions, drops unsubscribed events.
   137→
   138→**Configurable nodes** (opt-in per project, each with its own config schema):
   139→
   140→3. **Availability Checker** — Source-linked checks are automatic (Docker → verify image, GitHub → verify binaries). Config adds extra artifacts.
   141→4. **Risk Assessor** — Changelog keyword scanning is automatic. Config adds external signals (Discord, Telegram, GitHub Advisories).
   142→5. **Adoption Tracker** — Always requires config (provider + metrics source).
   143→6. **Changelog Summarizer** — Uses release changelog. Config can override LLM strategy.
   144→7. **Urgency Scorer** — Composite urgency from all preceding results. Config adjusts thresholds.
   145→8. **Validation Trigger** — Triggers SRE agent. Config sets urgency threshold.
   146→
   147→Every node implements a common `PipelineNode` interface and receives its config as raw JSON, which it unmarshals into its own typed struct. This means adding a new node or evolving a node's config requires no changes to the pipeline runner or database schema — just register the node and update the project's `pipeline_config`. The interface is designed to support **external plugin nodes** in the future — user-authored processing logic loaded via a plugin registry and executed over a transport layer (e.g., gRPC sidecar), with no changes to the runner or schema.
   148→
   149→The final notification is assembled as a fixed template with dynamic sections — each enabled configurable node maps to a section, and disabled nodes produce no section.
   150→
   151→### 2.5 Agentic Tooling
   152→
   153→For deep validation, ReleaseGuard utilizes SRE agents. The agent is provided a suite of abstracted tools:
   154→
   155→* `UpgradeBaseABoxConfig(version)`
   156→* `CheckAgentStatus(environment)`
   157→* `SyncOpsOpsack(payload)`
   158→This allows the agent to autonomously deploy a sandbox, verify that the deployment is healthy, and rollback or alert on failure.
   159→
   160→## 3. Data Flow: Lifecycle of a Release Event
   161→
   162→1. **Discovery:** A Go worker polling Docker Hub detects a new base image tag for a configured source (e.g., the "Docker Hub" source under the "Go Runtime" project). Source-level exclusion filters (`exclude_version_regexp`, `exclude_prereleases`) are applied — filtered versions are discarded immediately.
   163→2. **Ingestion & Transaction:** The worker standardizes the payload into a `ReleaseEvent` IR and executes a single database transaction to insert the record into `releases` (linked to its `source_id`) and queue a job in `pipeline_jobs`.
   164→3. **Processing (Queue Pull):** A Go worker pulls the job using `SKIP LOCKED` and routes the event through the DAG pipeline. Each node updates `current_node` and accumulates results in `node_results`. The subscription router checks the parent project's subscriptions — unsubscribed events are marked as `skipped`.
   165→4. **Analysis & Validation:** For stable releases, the LLM analyzes the release notes to generate a "Production Record" summary. If the urgency score meets a threshold, an SRE agent is triggered to draft a master config update for Base A Box and run automated health checks.
   166→5. **Finalization & Broadcast:** The worker updates the job status to `completed` in PostgreSQL. A Postgres trigger fires a `NOTIFY` payload to alert the routing matrix and push SSE events to connected dashboard clients.
   167→6. **Notification:** The Notification Matrix reads the finalized data, resolves the project's subscriptions, and routes to the appropriate notification channels (e.g., a critical PagerDuty alert for hotfixes via instant delivery, or a batched daily digest to Slack for low-priority updates).
   168→
   169→## 4. Directory Structure
   170→
   171→```text
   172→/releaseguard
   173→├── cmd/
   174→│   └── server/          # Main Go application entry point
   175→├── internal/
   176→│   ├── api/             # REST API handlers, middleware, SSE broadcaster
   177→│   ├── ingestion/       # Polling workers and webhook handlers (IIngestionSource)
   178→│   ├── pipeline/        # DAG node implementations for filtering & scoring
   179→│   ├── agents/          # LLM orchestration and validation tools
   180→│   ├── routing/         # Notification matrix and I/O providers (INotificationChannel)
   181→│   ├── queue/           # PostgreSQL River queue setup and job definitions
   182→│   ├── db/              # Connection pool and schema migrations
   183→│   └── models/          # Shared domain structs (ReleaseEvent, Project, etc.)
   184→├── web/                 # Next.js frontend application
   185→│   ├── app/
   186→│   └── components/
   187→├── deployments/         # Dockerfiles and Base A Box integration scripts
   188→└── go.mod
   189→
   190→```
```

     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→**ReleaseBeacon** (internal name: ReleaseGuard) is an event-driven release management system that polls upstream registries (Docker Hub, GitHub) for new releases, processes them through a DAG pipeline, and routes notifications to downstream systems (Slack, PagerDuty, Ops Opsack).
     8→
     9→Go module: `github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard`
    10→
    11→## Tech Stack
    12→
    13→- **Backend:** Go 1.25 — polling engine, DAG pipeline, API server
    14→- **Database/Queue/PubSub:** PostgreSQL + [River](https://github.com/riverqueue/river) v0.31.0 for job queue (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), native `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY` for real-time events
    15→- **Frontend:** Next.js (React) + Tailwind CSS — dashboard (not yet started, `web/` is empty)
    16→- **Intelligence:** LLMs (Gemini/GPT-4o-mini) via agent frameworks for changelog analysis and SRE validation (planned)
    17→- **Deployment:** Single binary — Go `//go:embed` serves Next.js static export
    18→
    19→## Build & Test Commands
    20→
    21→```bash
    22→# Build
    23→go build -o releaseguard ./cmd/server
    24→
    25→# Test all packages
    26→go test ./...
    27→
    28→# Test a single package
    29→go test ./internal/ingestion/...
    30→go test -v -run TestDockerHubSource ./internal/ingestion/...
    31→
    32→# Vet
    33→go vet ./...
    34→
    35→# Integration tests (requires Docker)
    36→bash scripts/integration-test.sh
    37→```
    38→
    39→## Environment Variables
    40→
    41→| Variable | Default | Purpose |
    42→|----------|---------|---------|
    43→| `DATABASE_URL` | `postgres://localhost:5432/releaseguard?sslmode=disable` | PostgreSQL connection |
    44→| `GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | (empty) | HMAC-SHA256 verification for GitHub webhooks |
    45→| `LISTEN_ADDR` | `:8080` | HTTP server bind address |
    46→
    47→## Architecture
    48→
    49→Four decoupled layers communicate exclusively through PostgreSQL:
    50→
    51→1. **Ingestion Layer** *(implemented)* — Polling workers and webhook handlers behind `IIngestionSource` interface
    52→2. **DAG Processing Pipeline** *(planned)* — Sequential nodes processing a `ReleaseEvent` IR
    53→3. **Agentic Validation** *(planned)* — SRE agent for autonomous sandbox testing
    54→4. **Routing & Notification** *(planned)* — Notification Matrix behind `INotificationChannel` interface
    55→
    56→### Transactional Outbox Pattern
    57→
    58→The critical data integrity pattern: release insert + River job enqueue happen in the same SQL transaction (`internal/ingestion/pgstore.go`). If either fails, both rollback — zero-loss guarantee.
    59→
    60→### Key Interfaces (implemented)
    61→
    62→- `IIngestionSource` (`internal/ingestion/source.go`) — Polling-based providers must implement `FetchLatest(ctx) ([]IngestionResult, error)`
    63→- `ReleaseStore` (`internal/ingestion/store.go`) — Persistence abstraction with `Save(ctx, source, results) error`
    64→
    65→### Key Interfaces (planned)
    66→
    67→- `PipelineNode` — DAG processing stages
    68→- `INotificationChannel` — Output providers (Slack, PagerDuty, webhooks)
    69→
    70→### Data Flow
    71→
    72→```
    73→IIngestionSource.FetchLatest() → IngestionResult
    74→    → IngestionService.ProcessResults() normalizes to ReleaseEvent IR
    75→    → PgStore.Save() in single TX: INSERT release + River InsertTx(PipelineJobArgs)
    76→    → River worker picks up job → DAG pipeline processes → notifications sent
    77→```
    78→
    79→## Database
    80→
    81→Schema lives in `internal/db/migrations.go` (idempotent, runs on startup). Current tables:
    82→- `releases` — unique on `(repository, version)` for idempotent ingestion
    83→- `subscriptions` — maps repositories to notification channels
    84→- River's own tables (created by `rivermigrate`)
    85→
    86→## Key Design References
    87→
    88→- `ARCH.md` — Full architecture: system diagram, data flow lifecycle, DAG pipeline design
    89→- `DESIGN.md` — Component design: ReleaseEvent IR struct, database schema, SRE agent workflow, error handling
    90→- `docs/designs/` — Feature-specific design docs (API design, etc.)
    91→- `docs/plans/` — Implementation plans and task tracking
    92→
    93→## Workflow Orchestration
    94→
    95→### 1. Plan Node Default
    96→- Enter plan mode for ANY non-trivial task (3+ steps or architectural decisions)
    97→- If something goes sideways, STOP and re-plan immediately — don't keep pushing
    98→- Use plan mode for verification steps, not just building
    99→- Write detailed specs upfront to reduce ambiguity
   100→
   101→### 2. Subagent Strategy
   102→- Use subagents liberally to keep main context window clean
   103→- Offload research, exploration, and parallel analysis to subagents
   104→- For complex problems, throw more compute at it via subagents
   105→- One task per subagent for focused execution
   106→
   107→### 3. Self-Improvement Loop
   108→- After ANY correction from the user: update `docs/plans/lessons.md` with the pattern
   109→- Write rules for yourself that prevent the same mistake
   110→- Ruthlessly iterate on these lessons until mistake rate drops
   111→- Review lessons at session start for relevant project
   112→
   113→### 4. Verification Before Done
   114→- Never mark a task complete without proving it works
   115→- Diff behavior between main and your changes when relevant
   116→- Ask yourself: "Would a staff engineer approve this?"
   117→- Run tests, check logs, demonstrate correctness
   118→
   119→### 5. Demand Elegance (Balanced)
   120→- For non-trivial changes: pause and ask "is there a more elegant way?"
   121→- If a fix feels hacky: "Knowing everything I know now, implement the elegant solution"
   122→- Skip this for simple, obvious fixes — don't over-engineer
   123→- Challenge your own work before presenting it
   124→
   125→### 6. Autonomous Bug Fixing
   126→- When given a bug report: just fix it. Don't ask for hand-holding
   127→- Point at logs, errors, failing tests — then resolve them
   128→- Zero context switching required from the user
   129→- Go fix failing CI tests without being told how
   130→
   131→## Task Management
   132→
   133→1. **Plan First**: Write plan to `docs/plans/todo.md` with checkable items
   134→2. **Verify Plan**: Check in before starting implementation
   135→3. **Track Progress**: Mark items complete as you go
   136→4. **Explain Changes**: High-level summary at each step
   137→5. **Document Results**: Add review section to `docs/plans/todo.md`
   138→6. **Capture Lessons**: Update `docs/plans/lessons.md` after corrections
   139→
   140→## Core Principles
   141→
   142→- **Simplicity First**: Make every change as simple as possible. Impact minimal code.
   143→- **No Laziness**: Find root causes. No temporary fixes. Senior developer standards.
   144→- **Minimal Impact**: Changes should only touch what's necessary. Avoid introducing bugs.
   145→
```

```
   270→## 4. Error Handling & Idempotency
   271→
   272→* **Idempotent Ingestion:** The `releases` table enforces a `UNIQUE(source_id, version)` constraint. If a polling worker and a webhook both report the same release from the same source simultaneously, the database rejects the duplicate, preventing duplicate pipeline jobs.
   273→* **Source-Level Filtering:** Before events enter the pipeline, the ingestion layer applies source-level exclusion rules (`exclude_version_regexp`, `exclude_prereleases`). Filtered releases are never inserted, reducing pipeline load.
   274→* **Dead-Letter Queue (DLQ):** If a DAG pipeline job fails `max_attempts` (e.g., the LLM API is down), the `pipeline_jobs` state is updated to `discarded` and `error_message` captures the failure reason. A separate Postgres trigger alerts the system admin that an event requires manual intervention.
   275→* **Pipeline Observability:** Each pipeline job tracks its `current_node` and accumulates `node_results` as it progresses through the DAG. If a job fails mid-pipeline, the exact failure point and partial results are preserved for debugging.
   276→* **Agent Fallback:** If the SRE Agent sandbox deployment fails due to infrastructure timeout (unrelated to the software release itself), the agent safely aborts the validation phase, flags the release event as "Unverified," and routes it to a human reviewer rather than dropping the notification.
   277→* **Notification Digest Fallback:** If a digest batch fails to send (e.g., Slack API outage), the batch is retried on the next interval. Individual items within the batch are not lost — they remain queued until the channel recovers.
```

```json
  "new_string": "* **Dead-Letter Queue (DLQ):** If a pipeline job fails `max_attempts` (e.g., the LLM API is down), the `pipeline_jobs` state is updated to `discarded` and `error_message` captures the failure reason. A separate Postgres trigger alerts the system admin that an event requires manual intervention.\n* **Pipeline Observability:** Each pipeline job tracks its `current_node` and accumulates `node_results` as it progresses through the pipeline. If a job fails mid-pipeline, the exact failure point and partial results are preserved for debugging."
}
```

```
     7→**Architecture:** River workers pull `pipeline_process` jobs. A `Runner` orchestrates sequential execution: always-on nodes (Regex Normalizer, Subscription Router) run for every release; configurable nodes (Urgency Scorer, etc.) run only when their key is present in `pipeline_config`. Each node implements the `PipelineNode` interface, receives prior results and config as raw JSON. A `pipeline_jobs` table tracks `current_node`, `node_results`, and `state` for dashboard observability. Without the `projects` table (not yet built), configurable nodes are dormant — only always-on nodes execute. Execution is sequential — nodes that are independent (e.g., Availability Checker, Risk Assessor) could be parallelized in a future DAG-based runner without changing the node interface.
```

    38→
    39→    Work from: [directory]
    40→
    41→    **While you work:** If you encounter something unexpected or unclear, **ask questions**.
    42→    It's always OK to pause and clarify. Don't guess or make assumptions.
    43→
    44→    ## Before Reporting Back: Self-Review
    45→
    46→    Review your work with fresh eyes. Ask yourself:
    47→
    48→    **Completeness:**
    49→    - Did I fully implement everything in the spec?
    50→    - Did I miss any requirements?
    51→    - Are there edge cases I didn't handle?
    52→
    53→    **Quality:**
    54→    - Is this my best work?
    55→    - Are names clear and accurate (match what things do, not how they work)?
    56→    - Is the code clean and maintainable?
    57→
    58→    **Discipline:**
    59→    - Did I avoid overbuilding (YAGNI)?
    60→    - Did I only build what was requested?
    61→    - Did I follow existing patterns in the codebase?
    62→
    63→    **Testing:**

    65→    - Did I follow TDD if required?
    66→    - Are tests comprehensive?
    67→
    68→    If you find issues during self-review, fix them now before reporting.
    69→

    71→

    73→    - What you implemented
    74→    - What you tested and test results
    75→    - Files changed
    76→    - Self-review findings (if any)
    77→    - Any issues or concerns

    25→

    49→    - Did they add "nice to haves" that weren't in spec?

```
     8→
     9→// ReleaseStore persists release events using the transactional outbox pattern.
    10→// The implementation inserts the release and enqueues a pipeline job atomically.
    11→type ReleaseStore interface {
    12→	IngestRelease(ctx context.Context, event *models.ReleaseEvent) error
```

id: toolu_01EizhsR66bL6v9XGKk2Tgbs
```
     1→package ingestion
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"encoding/json"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→
     8→	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
     9→	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
    10→	"github.com/riverqueue/river"
    11→	"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/models"
    12→	"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/queue"
    13→)
    14→
    15→// PgStore implements ReleaseStore using PostgreSQL + River for the transactional outbox.
    16→type PgStore struct {
    17→	pool  *pgxpool.Pool
    18→	river *river.Client[pgx.Tx]
    19→}
    20→
    21→func NewPgStore(pool *pgxpool.Pool, riverClient *river.Client[pgx.Tx]) *PgStore {
    22→	return &PgStore{pool: pool, river: riverClient}
    23→}
    24→
    25→// IngestRelease inserts a release and enqueues a pipeline job in a single transaction.
    26→// Returns an error on unique constraint violation (caller treats as idempotent skip).
    27→func (s *PgStore) IngestRelease(ctx context.Context, event *models.ReleaseEvent) error {
    28→	tx, err := s.pool.Begin(ctx)
    29→	if err != nil {
    30→		return fmt.Errorf("begin tx: %w", err)
    31→	}
    32→	defer tx.Rollback(ctx)
    33→
    34→	payload, err := json.Marshal(event)
    35→	if err != nil {
    36→		return fmt.Errorf("marshal payload: %w", err)
    37→	}
    38→
    39→	_, err = tx.Exec(ctx,
    40→		`INSERT INTO releases (id, source, repository, version, payload) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5)`,
    41→		event.ID, event.Source, event.Repository, event.RawVersion, payload,
    42→	)
    43→	if err != nil {
    44→		return fmt.Errorf("insert release: %w", err)
    45→	}
    46→
    47→	_, err = s.river.InsertTx(ctx, tx, queue.PipelineJobArgs{ReleaseID: event.ID}, nil)
    48→	if err != nil {
    49→		return fmt.Errorf("enqueue job: %w", err)
    50→	}
    51→
    52→	if err := tx.Commit(ctx); err != nil {
    53→		return fmt.Errorf("commit: %w", err)
    54→	}
    55→	return nil
    56→}
    57→
```

```
     1→package db
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"fmt"
     6→
     7→	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
     8→	"github.com/riverqueue/river/riverdriver/riverpgxv5"
     9→	"github.com/riverqueue/river/rivermigrate"
    10→)
    11→
    12→const schema = `
    13→-- Drop old-format tables (dev only — no production data exists)
    14→DROP TABLE IF EXISTS subscriptions CASCADE;
    15→DROP TABLE IF EXISTS releases CASCADE;
    16→
    17→-- Tracked software projects (the central entity)
    18→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS projects (
    19→    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    20→    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    21→    description TEXT,
    22→    url VARCHAR(500),
    23→    pipeline_config JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{"changelog_summarizer": {}, "urgency_scorer": {}}'::jsonb,
    24→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    25→    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
    26→);
    27→
    28→-- Configured ingestion sources
    29→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sources (
    30→    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    31→    project_id INT NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    32→    source_type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    33→    repository VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    34→    poll_interval_seconds INT DEFAULT 300,
    35→    enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
    36→    exclude_version_regexp TEXT,
    37→    exclude_prereleases BOOLEAN DEFAULT false,
    38→    last_polled_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    39→    last_error TEXT,
    40→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    41→    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    42→    UNIQUE(source_type, repository)
    43→);
    44→
    45→-- Normalized release events (references source, not raw strings)
    46→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS releases (
    47→    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    48→    source_id INT NOT NULL REFERENCES sources(id),
    49→    version VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    50→    payload JSONB NOT NULL,
    51→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    52→    UNIQUE(source_id, version)
    53→);
    54→
    55→-- Pipeline job tracking (application-level, separate from River internals)
    56→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pipeline_jobs (
    57→    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    58→    state VARCHAR(50) DEFAULT 'available',
    59→    release_id UUID REFERENCES releases(id),
    60→    current_node VARCHAR(50),
    61→    node_results JSONB DEFAULT '{}',
    62→    attempt INT DEFAULT 0,
    63→    max_attempts INT DEFAULT 3,
    64→    error_message TEXT,
    65→    locked_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    66→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    67→    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ
    68→);
    69→
    70→-- Registered notification channels
    71→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS notification_channels (
    72→    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    73→    type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    74→    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    75→    config JSONB NOT NULL,
    76→    enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
    77→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
    78→);
    79→
    80→-- Subscriptions: route project releases to notification channels
    81→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS subscriptions (
    82→    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    83→    project_id INT NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    84→    channel_type VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    85→    channel_id INT REFERENCES notification_channels(id),
    86→    version_pattern TEXT,
    87→    frequency VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'instant',
    88→    enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,
    89→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    90→    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
    91→);
    92→
    93→-- API authentication keys
    94→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS api_keys (
    95→    id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    96→    name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    97→    key_hash VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    98→    key_prefix VARCHAR(12) NOT NULL,
    99→    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
   100→    last_used_at TIMESTAMPTZ
   101→);
   102→
   103→-- Trigger for SSE: notify on new releases
   104→CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION notify_release_created() RETURNS trigger AS $$
   105→BEGIN
   106→    PERFORM pg_notify('release_events', NEW.id::text);
   107→    RETURN NEW;
   108→END;
   109→$$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;
   110→
   111→DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS release_created_trigger ON releases;
   112→CREATE TRIGGER release_created_trigger
   113→    AFTER INSERT ON releases
   114→    FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION notify_release_created();
   115→`
   116→
   117→// RunMigrations applies River's schema and the application schema. Idempotent — safe to call on every startup.
   118→func RunMigrations(ctx context.Context, pool *pgxpool.Pool) error {
   119→	migrator, err := rivermigrate.New(riverpgxv5.New(pool), nil)
   120→	if err != nil {
   121→		return fmt.Errorf("create river migrator: %w", err)
   122→	}
   123→	if _, err := migrator.Migrate(ctx, rivermigrate.DirectionUp, nil); err != nil {
   124→		return fmt.Errorf("river migrations: %w", err)
   125→	}
   126→
   127→	if _, err := pool.Exec(ctx, schema); err != nil {
   128→		return fmt.Errorf("app migrations: %w", err)
   129→	}
   130→	return nil
   131→}
   132→
```

```
     1→package ingestion
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"log/slog"
     6→
     7→	"github.com/google/uuid"
     8→	"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/models"
     9→)
    10→
    11→// Service orchestrates ingestion sources and persists normalized results.
    12→type Service struct {
    13→	store ReleaseStore
    14→}
    15→
    16→func NewService(store ReleaseStore) *Service {
    17→	return &Service{store: store}
    18→}
    19→
    20→// ProcessResults normalizes raw ingestion results into ReleaseEvents and persists them.
    21→// Duplicate releases (unique constraint violations) are logged and skipped, not fatal.
    22→func (s *Service) ProcessResults(ctx context.Context, sourceName string, results []IngestionResult) error {
    23→	for _, r := range results {
    24→		event := &models.ReleaseEvent{
    25→			ID:         uuid.New().String(),
    26→			Source:     sourceName,
    27→			Repository: r.Repository,
    28→			RawVersion: r.RawVersion,
    29→			Changelog:  r.Changelog,
    30→			Metadata:   r.Metadata,
    31→			Timestamp:  r.Timestamp,
    32→		}
    33→
    34→		if err := s.store.IngestRelease(ctx, event); err != nil {
    35→			slog.Warn("ingest failed (may be duplicate)",
    36→				"repo", r.Repository, "version", r.RawVersion, "err", err)
    37→			continue
    38→		}
    39→	}
    40→	return nil
    41→}
    42→
```

```
## Summary

Task 2 (Pipeline Node Interface) is complete. Here is what was done:

### Files Created

1. **`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/.worktrees/configurable-pipeline/internal/pipeline/node.go`** -- The `PipelineNode` interface and `ErrEventDropped` sentinel error.

2. **`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/.worktrees/configurable-pipeline/internal/pipeline/node_test.go`** -- Test verifying `ErrEventDropped` is identifiable via `errors.Is`.

### What Was Implemented

- **`ErrEventDropped`** -- A sentinel error (`errors.New("event dropped by pipeline node")`) that pipeline nodes return when they decide an event should be dropped. The pipeline runner will use `errors.Is` to detect this and mark the job as "skipped".

- **`PipelineNode` interface** -- The contract all pipeline processing nodes must implement:
  - `Name() string` -- Returns the node identifier (e.g., `"regex_normalizer"`)
  - `Execute(ctx, event, config, prior) (json.RawMessage, error)` -- Processes a `*models.ReleaseEvent` with optional per-node config and results from previously executed nodes, returning a JSON result
```

     1→# Configurable Pipeline Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.
     4→
     5→**Goal:** Build the configurable processing pipeline that consumes River jobs, runs release events through sequential processing nodes (version normalization, subscription routing, urgency scoring), tracks progress in a `pipeline_jobs` table, and is wired into `main.go` as a River worker.
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** River workers pull `pipeline_process` jobs. A `Runner` orchestrates sequential execution: always-on nodes (Regex Normalizer, Subscription Router) run for every release; configurable nodes (Urgency Scorer, etc.) run only when their key is present in `pipeline_config`. Each node implements the `PipelineNode` interface, receives prior results and config as raw JSON. A `pipeline_jobs` table tracks `current_node`, `node_results`, and `state` for dashboard observability. Without the `projects` table (not yet built), configurable nodes are dormant — only always-on nodes execute. Execution is sequential — nodes that are independent (e.g., Availability Checker, Risk Assessor) could be parallelized in a future DAG-based runner without changing the node interface.
     8→
     9→**Tech Stack:** Go 1.25, PostgreSQL (pgx/v5), River v0.31.0, regexp, encoding/json
    10→

   158→## Task 3: Pipeline Store Interface + PostgreSQL Implementation
   159→
   160→**Files:**
   161→- Create: `internal/pipeline/store.go`
   162→- Create: `internal/pipeline/pgstore.go`
   163→
   164→No unit tests — this is a thin infrastructure adapter (same pattern as `internal/ingestion/pgstore.go`). Covered by integration tests.
   165→
   166→**Step 1: Define the store interface**
   167→
   168→```go
   169→// internal/pipeline/store.go
   170→package pipeline
   171→
   172→import (
   173→	"context"
   174→	"encoding/json"
   175→
   176→	"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/models"
   177→)
   178→
   179→// Store provides data access for the pipeline runner.
   180→type Store interface {
   181→	GetReleasePayload(ctx context.Context, releaseID string) (*models.ReleaseEvent, error)
   182→	UpsertPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, releaseID string) (int64, error)
   183→	UpdateNodeProgress(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, currentNode string, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error
   184→	CompletePipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error
   185→	SkipPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, reason string) error
   186→	FailPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, errMsg string) error
   187→}
   188→```
   189→
   190→**Step 2: Implement PgStore**
   191→
   192→```go
   193→// internal/pipeline/pgstore.go
   194→package pipeline
   195→
   196→import (
   197→	"context"
   198→	"encoding/json"
   199→	"fmt"
   200→
   201→	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
   202→	"github.com/sentioxyz/releaseguard/internal/models"
   203→)
   204→
   205→// PgStore implements Store and SubscriptionChecker using PostgreSQL.
   206→type PgStore struct {
   207→	pool *pgxpool.Pool
   208→}
   209→
   210→func NewPgStore(pool *pgxpool.Pool) *PgStore {
   211→	return &PgStore{pool: pool}
   212→}
   213→
   214→func (s *PgStore) GetReleasePayload(ctx context.Context, releaseID string) (*models.ReleaseEvent, error) {
   215→	var payload []byte
   216→	err := s.pool.QueryRow(ctx, `SELECT payload FROM releases WHERE id = $1`, releaseID).Scan(&payload)
   217→	if err != nil {
   218→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("get release: %w", err)
   219→	}
   220→	var event models.ReleaseEvent
   221→	if err := json.Unmarshal(payload, &event); err != nil {
   222→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unmarshal payload: %w", err)
   223→	}
   224→	return &event, nil
   225→}
   226→
   227→func (s *PgStore) UpsertPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, releaseID string) (int64, error) {
   228→	var id int64
   229→	err := s.pool.QueryRow(ctx, `
   230→		INSERT INTO pipeline_jobs (release_id, state)
   231→		VALUES ($1, 'running')
   232→		ON CONFLICT (release_id) DO UPDATE SET
   233→			state = 'running',
   234→			current_node = NULL,
   235→			node_results = '{}',
   236→			error_message = NULL,
   237→			completed_at = NULL
   238→		RETURNING id
   239→	`, releaseID).Scan(&id)
   240→	if err != nil {
   241→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("upsert pipeline job: %w", err)
   242→	}
   243→	return id, nil
   244→}
   245→
   246→func (s *PgStore) UpdateNodeProgress(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, currentNode string, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error {
   247→	resultsJSON, err := json.Marshal(nodeResults)
   248→	if err != nil {
   249→		return fmt.Errorf("marshal results: %w", err)
   250→	}
   251→	_, err = s.pool.Exec(ctx,
   252→		`UPDATE pipeline_jobs SET current_node = $2, node_results = $3 WHERE id = $1`,
   253→		jobID, currentNode, resultsJSON,
   254→	)
   255→	return err
   256→}
   257→
   258→func (s *PgStore) CompletePipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, nodeResults map[string]json.RawMessage) error {
   259→	resultsJSON, err := json.Marshal(nodeResults)
   260→	if err != nil {
   261→		return fmt.Errorf("marshal results: %w", err)
   262→	}
   263→	_, err = s.pool.Exec(ctx,
   264→		`UPDATE pipeline_jobs SET state = 'completed', current_node = NULL, node_results = $2, completed_at = NOW() WHERE id = $1`,
   265→		jobID, resultsJSON,
   266→	)
   267→	return err
   268→}
   269→
   270→func (s *PgStore) SkipPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, reason string) error {
   271→	_, err := s.pool.Exec(ctx,
   272→		`UPDATE pipeline_jobs SET state = 'skipped', error_message = $2, completed_at = NOW() WHERE id = $1`,
   273→		jobID, reason,
   274→	)
   275→	return err
   276→}
   277→
   278→func (s *PgStore) FailPipelineJob(ctx context.Context, jobID int64, errMsg string) error {
   279→	_, err := s.pool.Exec(ctx,
   280→		`UPDATE pipeline_jobs SET state = 'failed', error_message = $2, completed_at = NOW() WHERE id = $1`,
   281→		jobID, errMsg,
   282→	)
   283→	return err
   284→}
   285→
   286→// HasSubscribers returns true if the repository has active subscriptions,
   287→// or if no subscriptions exist at all (default open — system unconfigured).
   288→func (s *PgStore) HasSubscribers(ctx context.Context, repository string) (bool, error) {
   289→	var result bool
   290→	err := s.pool.QueryRow(ctx, `
   291→		SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM subscriptions WHERE repository = $1)
   292→			OR NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM subscriptions)
   293→	`, repository).Scan(&result)
   294→	if err != nil {
   295→		return false, fmt.Errorf("check subscribers: %w", err)
   296→	}
   297→	return result, nil
   298→}
   299→```
   300→

   304→Expected: Success
